<div class="marriage-display-ws"><div style="display:inline-block;line-height:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Chennamma_Deve_Gowda" class="mw-redirect" title="Chennamma Deve Gowda">Chennamma</a></div>&#32;<div style="display:inline-block;">&#8203;</div>&#40;<abbr title="married">m.</abbr>&#160;<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1038841319">.mw-parser-output .tooltip-dotted{border-bottom:1px dotted;cursor:help}</style><span class="rt-commentedText tooltip" title="25 May 1954">1954</span>&#41;<wbr />&#8203;</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Children</th><td class="infobox-data">6 children; including <a href="/wiki/H._D._Revanna" title="H. Polytechnic, Hassan</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Profession</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Politician" title="Politician">Politician</a>, <a href="/wiki/Farmer" title="Farmer">Farmer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Civil_engineer" title="Civil engineer">Civil engineer</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Signature</th><td class="infobox-data"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:DeveGowda_autograph.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="H. Deve Gowda&#39;s signature"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/DeveGowda_autograph.jpg/125px-DeveGowda_autograph.jpg" decoding="async" width="125" height="80" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/DeveGowda_autograph.jpg/187px-DeveGowda_autograph.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/DeveGowda_autograph.jpg/249px-DeveGowda_autograph.jpg 2x" data-file-width="581" data-file-height="374" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Website</th><td class="infobox-data"><span class="url"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://hddevegowda.in">hddevegowda<wbr />.in</a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Nickname(s)</th><td class="infobox-data">Mannina Maga <br /> Dodda Gowdaru</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Haradanahalli Doddegowda Deve Gowda</b> (<span style="font-size:85%;"><span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-labelElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/5\/5b\/H._D._Deve_Gowda_name_pronounciation.ogg\/H._D._Deve_Gowda_name_pronounciation.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;label&quot;:{&quot;html&quot;:&quot;pronunciation&quot;},&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;H. D. Deve Gowda name pronounciation.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/5b/H._D._Deve_Gowda_name_pronounciation.ogg/H._D._Deve_Gowda_name_pronounciation.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label">pronunciation</span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:H._D._Deve_Gowda_name_pronounciation.ogg" title="File:H. D. Deve Gowda name pronounciation.ogg">i</a></sup></span>; born 18 May 1933)<sup id="cite_ref-pmindia1_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia1-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> is an Indian politician who served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of India">11th</a> <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">prime minister of India</a> from 1 June 1996 to 21 April 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-www.britannica.com_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www.britannica.com-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He was previously the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Chief_Ministers_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Chief Ministers of Karnataka">14th</a> <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Karnataka">Chief Minister of Karnataka</a> from 1994 to 1996. He presently is a <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament,_Lok_Sabha" title="Member of Parliament, Lok Sabha">Member of Parliament</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> representing <a href="/wiki/Karnataka" title="Karnataka">Karnataka</a>. He is the national president of the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal_(Secular)" title="Janata Dal (Secular)">Janata Dal (Secular)</a> party.<sup id="cite_ref-janata.in_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-janata.in-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Born in a farming family, he joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party in 1953, and remained a member until 1962. He was imprisoned during <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">the Emergency</a>. He became President of the state unit of <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> in 1994, and was considered to be a driving force in the party's victory in <a href="/wiki/Karnataka" title="Karnataka">Karnataka</a>. He served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Chief_Ministers_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Chief Ministers of Karnataka">8th</a> <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Karnataka">Chief Minister of Karnataka</a> from 1994 to 1996. In the <a href="/wiki/1996_Indian_general_election" title="1996 Indian general election">1996 general elections</a>, no party won enough seats to form a government. When the <a href="/wiki/Third_Front_(India)" title="Third Front (India)">United Front</a>, a coalition of regional parties, formed the central government with the support of the Congress, Deve Gowda was unexpectedly chosen to head the government and was elected Prime Minister. During his tenure as prime minister, he also served as Home Minister for some time. His prime ministerial tenure lasted for less than a year. After his prime ministerial tenure, he was elected to the 12th (1998), 14th (2004), <a href="/wiki/15th_Lok_Sabha" title="15th Lok Sabha">15th</a>, and <a href="/wiki/16th_Lok_Sabha" title="16th Lok Sabha">16th Lok Sabha</a>, as Member of Parliament for the <a href="/wiki/Hassan_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hassan (Lok Sabha constituency)">Hassan</a> Lok Sabha constituency. He lost <a href="/wiki/2019_Indian_general_election" title="2019 Indian general election">Lok Sabha elections in 2019</a> from <a href="/wiki/Tumkur_Lok_Sabha_constituency" title="Tumkur Lok Sabha constituency">Tumkuru</a> but has been elected to Rajya Sabha since.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>H. D. Deve Gowda was born on 18 May 1933 in <a href="/wiki/Haradanahalli" title="Haradanahalli">Haradanahalli</a>, a village in <a href="/wiki/Holenarasipura" title="Holenarasipura">Holenarasipura</a> <a href="/wiki/Taluk" class="mw-redirect" title="Taluk">Taluk</a>, of the erstwhile <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Mysore" title="Kingdom of Mysore">Kingdom of Mysore</a> (now in <a href="/wiki/Hassan_district" title="Hassan district">Hassan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Karnataka" title="Karnataka">Karnataka</a>). His father Dodde Gowda was a <a href="/wiki/Paddy_(unmilled_rice)" class="mw-redirect" title="Paddy (unmilled rice)">paddy</a> farmer and mother, Devamma was a home maker.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gowda earned a diploma in <a href="/wiki/Civil_engineering" title="Civil engineering">civil engineering</a> from L. V. Polytechnic, Hassan, in the early 1950s.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg/220px-Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg/330px-Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg/440px-Deve_Gowda_and_Manmohan_Singh.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1900" data-file-height="1258" /></a><figcaption>Deve Gowda and <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Narendra_Modi,_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11,_2015.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11%2C_2015.jpg/220px-The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11%2C_2015.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11%2C_2015.jpg/330px-The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11%2C_2015.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11%2C_2015.jpg/440px-The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_calls_on_the_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_11%2C_2015.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1628" /></a><figcaption>Deve Gowda and <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a></figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Gowda joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> party in 1953 and remained a member until 1962. During that period, he was President of Anjaneya Cooperative Society of Holenarasipura and later became a member of the Taluk Development Board of Holenarasipura.
</p><p>In 1962, Gowda was elected to the <a href="/wiki/Karnataka_Legislative_Assembly" title="Karnataka Legislative Assembly">Karnataka Legislative Assembly</a> from Holenarasipura constituency as an <a href="/wiki/Independent_politician" title="Independent politician">independent candidate</a>. Later, he was elected from the same constituency to the Assembly for six consecutive terms from 1962 to 1989. He joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress_(Organisation)" title="Indian National Congress (Organisation)">Congress (O)</a> during the Congress split and served as the Leader of Opposition in the Assembly from March 1972 to March 1976 and again from November 1976 to December 1977.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">Emergency</a> (1975–77), he was imprisoned in the Bangalore Central Jail.
</p><p>Gowda was the two time President of state unit of the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>. He continued to win from Holenarasipur assembly segment on Janata Party's ticket in 1978, 1983 and 1985. He served as a minister in the Janata Party Government in Karnataka headed by <a href="/wiki/Ramakrishna_Hegde" title="Ramakrishna Hegde">Ramakrishna Hegde</a> from 1983 to 1988. When V P Singh joined Janata Dal, Subramanian Swamy formed Janata Party (Jaya Prakash) faction, and Deve Gowda joined him to become Janata Party (JP)'s Karnataka President. But he lost from Holenarasipur in 1989, and soon later rejoined <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> He became President of the state unit of <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> in 1994 and was the driving force behind the victory of the party in the <a href="/wiki/1994_Karnataka_Legislative_Assembly_election" title="1994 Karnataka Legislative Assembly election">1994 State Assembly elections</a>. He was elected from the Ramanagara, and sworn in as the 14th <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Karnataka">Chief Minister of Karnataka</a> in December.
</p><p>In January 1995, Gowda toured <a href="/wiki/Switzerland" title="Switzerland">Switzerland</a> and attended the Forum of International Economists. His tour to <a href="/wiki/Singapore" title="Singapore">Singapore</a> brought in foreign investment to the State.<sup id="cite_ref-pmindia1_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia1-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister">Prime Minister</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Prime Minister">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Deve_Gowda_ministry" title="Deve Gowda ministry">Deve Gowda ministry</a></div>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/1996_Indian_general_election" title="1996 Indian general election">1996 general elections</a>, the Congress party headed by <a href="/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao" title="P. V. Narasimha Rao">P. V. Narasimha Rao</a> lost decisively but no other party won enough seats to form a government.
</p><p>When the <a href="/wiki/United_Front_(India)" title="United Front (India)">United Front</a> (a conglomeration of non-Congress and non-BJP regional parties) decided to form the Government at the Centre with the support of the Congress, Deve Gowda was unexpectedly chosen to head the government and became the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of India">11th</a> <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-janata.in_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-janata.in-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> He took over as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a> on 1 June 1996 and continued until 21 April 1997. Also, he was the Chairman of the Steering Committee of the United Front, the policy making apex body of all the constituents of the ruling front.<sup id="cite_ref-janata.in_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-janata.in-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> He is credited for providing financial closure and kickstarting development of the <a href="/wiki/Delhi_Metro" title="Delhi Metro">Delhi Metro</a> Project.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-premiership">Post-premiership</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Post-premiership">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal_(Secular)" title="Janata Dal (Secular)">Janata Dal (Secular)</a> traces its roots back to the Janata Dal founded by <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> was formed on the merger of the Janata Party with smaller opposition parties in 1988. <a href="/wiki/Vishwanath_Pratap_Singh" class="mw-redirect" title="Vishwanath Pratap Singh">Vishwanath Pratap Singh</a> became the first <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a> from Janata Dal when he headed the <a href="/wiki/National_Front_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="National Front (India)">National Front</a> government in 1989. Later Deve Gowda and <a href="/wiki/Inder_Kumar_Gujral" title="Inder Kumar Gujral">Inder Kumar Gujral</a> too became prime ministers heading the United Front (UF) coalition governments in 1996 and 1997 respectively.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 1999, when some senior leaders of the party decided to join hands with the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP)-led NDA, the party split into factions. Many leaders, including <a href="/wiki/Madhu_Dandavate" title="Madhu Dandavate">Madhu Dandawate</a> and <a href="/wiki/Siddaramaiah" title="Siddaramaiah">Siddaramaiah</a>, joined the Janata Dal (Secular) faction headed by Deve Gowda, who became the National president of this faction.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>He was defeated in the <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_India" title="Elections in India">1999 general elections</a>.
</p><p>The 2004 elections in Karnataka witnessed the revival of his party's fortunes under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Siddaramaiah" title="Siddaramaiah">Siddaramaiah</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal_(Secular)" title="Janata Dal (Secular)">Janata Dal (Secular)</a> winning 58 seats and becoming a part of the ruling coalition in the state. Later, the party joined with the BJP and formed an alternate government in 2006. His son <a href="/wiki/H._D._Kumaraswamy" title="H. D. Kumaraswamy">H. D. Kumaraswamy</a> headed the BJP-JD(S) coalition government in the state for 20 months.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> In the 2008 state elections, the party performed poorly and won just 28 seats, but it has remained a significant force in South Karnataka.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Deve Gowda expelled <a href="/wiki/Siddaramaiah" title="Siddaramaiah">Siddaramaiah</a> and <a href="/wiki/CM_Ibrahim" class="mw-redirect" title="CM Ibrahim">CM Ibrahim</a> JDS party, because Siddaramaiah led <a href="/wiki/AHINDA" class="mw-redirect" title="AHINDA">AHINDA</a><sup id="cite_ref-Ahinda1_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahinda1-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ahinda2_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahinda2-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ahinda3_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahinda3-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> movement; representing minority, <a href="/wiki/Other_Backward_Class" title="Other Backward Class">backward</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dalit" title="Dalit">Dalit</a> people in Karnataka.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Hindu_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Hindu-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Later, both <a href="/wiki/Siddaramaiah" title="Siddaramaiah">Siddaramaiah</a> and <a href="/wiki/CM_Ibrahim" class="mw-redirect" title="CM Ibrahim">CM Ibrahim</a> joined the Indian National Congress,<sup id="cite_ref-Siddaramaiah_joins_Congress_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Siddaramaiah_joins_Congress-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> which won the 2013 Vidhana Sabha election. <a href="/wiki/Siddaramaiah" title="Siddaramaiah">Siddaramaiah</a> was elected as the Chief Minister of Karnataka state in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-NDTV.com_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NDTV.com-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2008, JDS did not transfer the power to BJP with <a href="/wiki/B._S._Yediyurappa" title="B. S. Yediyurappa">B. S. Yediyurappa</a> as CM in accord to the initial negotiation.<sup id="cite_ref-BJP&#39;s_assessment_of_JD(S)_was_wrong:_Venkaiah_Naidu_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BJP&#39;s_assessment_of_JD(S)_was_wrong:_Venkaiah_Naidu-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-JDS_did_not_betray_BJP,_says_Kumaraswamy_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JDS_did_not_betray_BJP,_says_Kumaraswamy-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BJP_says_its_Betrayal_withdraws_Support_to_JDS_in_Karnataka_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BJP_says_its_Betrayal_withdraws_Support_to_JDS_in_Karnataka-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> This led to major setback for JDS in 2008 vidhana sabha election, JDS received only 28 seats<sup id="cite_ref-Karnataka_Assembly_Election_Results_2008_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Karnataka_Assembly_Election_Results_2008-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> compared to 58 seats<sup id="cite_ref-Karnataka_Assembly_Election_Results_2004_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Karnataka_Assembly_Election_Results_2004-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> in the 2004 vidhana sabha election. Since B. S. Yediyurappa is from <a href="/wiki/Lingayatism" title="Lingayatism">Lingayath</a> community, largest in the Karnataka state, many leaders in JDS from <a href="/wiki/Lingayatism" title="Lingayatism">Lingayath</a> community such as <a href="/wiki/M.P._Prakash" class="mw-redirect" title="M.P. Prakash">M. P. Prakash</a> quit the party.<sup id="cite_ref-Monsters_and_Critics_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Monsters_and_Critics-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> B. S. Yediyurappa was elected as the Chief Minister of Karnataka state in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-Oneindia_News_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Oneindia_News-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Deve Gowda abused B. S. Yediyurappa, who was then <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Karnataka">chief minister of Karnataka</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-abuse1_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abuse1-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-abuse3_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abuse3-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> This event was termed as "new low in Indian politics".<sup id="cite_ref-abuse4_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abuse4-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Gowda later apologised for hurling abuse at the chief minister of Karnataka.<sup id="cite_ref-abuse2_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abuse2-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Deve Gowda contested the <a href="/wiki/2019_Indian_general_election" title="2019 Indian general election">2019 general elections</a> against <a href="/wiki/G._S._Basavaraj" title="G. S. Basavaraj">G. S. Basavaraj</a> in <a href="/wiki/Tumkur_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tumkur (Lok Sabha constituency)">Tumkur Lok Sabha constituency</a> of <a href="/wiki/Karnataka" title="Karnataka">Karnataka</a>. <a href="/wiki/G._S._Basavaraj" title="G. S. Basavaraj">G. S. Basavaraj</a>, BJP candidate of Tumkur Constituency won against Deve Gowda by a margin of 13,339 votes. <a href="/wiki/G._S._Basavaraj" title="G. S. Basavaraj">G. S. Basavaraj</a> polled 5,96,127 votes while Deve Gowda got 5,82,788 votes.<sup id="cite_ref-Siddaramaiah_sworn_in_as_Karnataka_Chief_Minister_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Siddaramaiah_sworn_in_as_Karnataka_Chief_Minister-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>He married <a href="/wiki/Chennamma_Deve_Gowda" class="mw-redirect" title="Chennamma Deve Gowda">Chennamma</a> in 1954. They have six children together: four sons, including politicians <a href="/wiki/H._D._Revanna" title="H. D. Revanna">H. D. Revanna</a> and <a href="/wiki/H._D._Kumaraswamy" title="H. D. Kumaraswamy">H. D. Kumaraswamy</a>, who is the former <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Karnataka">Chief Minister of Karnataka</a>, and two daughters.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Electoral_history">Electoral history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Electoral history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<td><sup id="cite_ref-elections.in_hassan_37-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elections.in_hassan-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>2019</td>
<td><a href="/wiki/Tumkur_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tumkur (Lok Sabha constituency)">Tumkur</a></td>
<td><a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal_(Secular)" title="Janata Dal (Secular)">JD(S)</a></td>
<td style="background:#FFC7C7; vertical-align: middle; text-align: center;" class="table-no">Lost</td>

<td><a href="/wiki/G._S._Basavaraj" title="G. S. Basavaraj">G. S. Basavaraj</a></td>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Positions_held">Positions held</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Positions held">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Importance_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Ambox_important.svg/40px-Ambox_important.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="40" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Ambox_important.svg/60px-Ambox_important.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Ambox_important.svg/80px-Ambox_important.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="40" data-file-height="40" /></span></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>contains information of unclear or questionable <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:What_Wikipedia_is_not#Encyclopedic_content" title="Wikipedia:What Wikipedia is not">importance</a> or <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Writing_better_articles" title="Wikipedia:Writing better articles">relevance</a> to the article's subject.</b><span class="hide-when-compact"> Please <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=H._D._Deve_Gowda&amp;action=edit">help improve</a> this section by clarifying or removing <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:What_Wikipedia_is_not#Wikipedia_is_not_an_indiscriminate_collection_of_information" title="Wikipedia:What Wikipedia is not">indiscriminate details</a>. <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_addressing_at_the_release_of_Book_%E2%80%9CMOVING_ON%E2%80%A6_MOVING_FORWARD_A_YEAR_IN_OFFICE%E2%80%9D,_published_on_the_completion_of_One_Year_in_the_Office_of_the_Vice_President.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_addressing_at_the_release_of_Book_%E2%80%9CMOVING_ON%E2%80%A6_MOVING_FORWARD_A_YEAR_IN_OFFICE%E2%80%9D%2C_published_on_the_completion_of_One_Year_in_the_Office_of_the_Vice_President.JPG/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_addressing_at_the_release_of_Book_%E2%80%9CMOVING_ON%E2%80%A6_MOVING_FORWARD_A_YEAR_IN_OFFICE%E2%80%9D%2C_published_on_the_completion_of_One_Year_in_the_Office_of_the_Vice_President.JPG/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/The_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_H.D._Deve_Gowda_addressing_at_the_release_of_Book_%E2%80%9CMOVING_ON%E2%80%A6_MOVING_FORWARD_A_YEAR_IN_OFFICE%E2%80%9D%2C_published_on_the_completion_of_One_Year_in_the_Office_of_the_Vice_President.JPG/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="2273" /></a><figcaption>Deve Gowda giving a speech</figcaption></figure>
<ul><li>1962–1989, 1994–1996&#160;: Member, <a href="/wiki/Karnataka_Legislative_Assembly" title="Karnataka Legislative Assembly">Karnataka Legislative Assembly</a></li>
<li>1972–1976&#160;: Leader of Opposition, Karnataka Legislative Assembly.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1978&#160;: Member, <a href="/wiki/Karnataka_Legislative_Assembly" title="Karnataka Legislative Assembly">Karnataka Legislative Assembly</a>, from Holenarasipur</li>
<li>1983–1988&#160;: Minister for Public Works and Irrigation, Government of Karnataka.</li>
<li>1989&#160;: Karnataka State President of the new Janata Party (Jaya Prakash) faction. <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a> was the national president.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1991&#160;: Elected to <a href="/wiki/10th_Lok_Sabha" title="10th Lok Sabha">10th Lok Sabha</a> from <a href="/wiki/Hassan_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hassan (Lok Sabha constituency)">Hassan (Lok Sabha constituency)</a></li>
<li>1991–1994&#160;: Member, Committee on Commerce
<ul><li>Member, Joint Parliamentary Committee on Fertilizers</li>
<li>Member, Consultative Committee, Ministry of Agriculture</li></ul></li>
<li>1994&#160;: President, <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>, Karnataka.</li>
<li>1994–1996&#160;: <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Karnataka" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister of Karnataka">Chief Minister of Karnataka</a>,</li>
<li>Jun. 1996 – Apr. 1997&#160;: <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of India">Prime Minister of India</a> and also in charge of Ministries/Departments of Petroleum and Chemicals, Personnel, Public Grievances and Pensions, Atomic Energy, Home Affairs, Agriculture, Food Processing Industries, Urban Affairs and Employment and Non-Conventional Energy Sources</li>
<li>1996–1998&#160;: Member, <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a></li>
<li>Nov. 1996-Apr. 1997&#160;: Leader of the House, <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a></li>
<li>1998&#160;: Re-elected to <a href="/wiki/12th_Lok_Sabha" title="12th Lok Sabha">12th Lok Sabha</a> (2nd term).
<ul><li>National President, <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal_(Secular)" title="Janata Dal (Secular)">Janata Dal (Secular)</a><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li>
<li>2002&#160;: Re-elected to <a href="/wiki/13th_Lok_Sabha" title="13th Lok Sabha">13th Lok Sabha</a> winning a by-election from <a href="/wiki/Kanakapura_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Kanakapura (Lok Sabha constituency)">Kanakpura</a>. (3rd term)</li>
<li>2004&#160;: Contested elections for <a href="/wiki/14th_Lok_Sabha" title="14th Lok Sabha">14th Lok Sabha</a> from two seats.
<ul><li>Was elected to <a href="/wiki/14th_Lok_Sabha" title="14th Lok Sabha">14th Lok Sabha</a> (4th term), from Hassan</li>
<li>But lost from <a href="/wiki/Kanakapura_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Kanakapura (Lok Sabha constituency)">Kanakpura</a>, where he came third behind the winner <a href="/wiki/Tejashwini_Gowda" title="Tejashwini Gowda">Tejashwini Gowda</a> (Congress) and <a href="/wiki/Ramachandra_Gowda" title="Ramachandra Gowda">Ramachandra Gowda</a> (BJP).<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li>
<li>2006–2008&#160;: Member, Committee on Railways</li>
<li>2009&#160;: Re-elected to <a href="/wiki/15th_Lok_Sabha" title="15th Lok Sabha">15th Lok Sabha</a> (5th term)</li>
<li>31 Aug 2009&#160;: Member, Committee on Defence</li>
<li>2014&#160;: Re-elected to <a href="/wiki/16th_Lok_Sabha" title="16th Lok Sabha">16th Lok Sabha</a> (6th term)</li>
<li>1 Sep 2014&#160;: Appointed member, Standing Committee on Defence</li>
<li>23 May 2019: Lost from <a href="/wiki/Tumkur_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tumkur (Lok Sabha constituency)">Tumkur</a> in <a href="/wiki/2019_Indian_general_election" title="2019 Indian general election">17th Lok Sabha elections</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164.100.47.132_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164.100.47.132-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> This was the third time he lost a <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a> election as a former Prime Minister, after defeats in 1999 and 2004.</li>
<li>2020&#160;: Elected to <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> from Karnataka.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
